- **cross-member-metabolic-dependency-resolution**: Use when when you have consensus metabolic reconstructions for multiple community members (e. - **cross-method-accuracy-benchmarking**: Use when evaluating a scRNA-seq cell-type annotation method head-to-head with published baselines under multiple label-overlap settings, and accuracy values must be matched against a reference table. - **cross-method-accuracy-comparison**: Use when a new cell type annotation method must be quantitatively compared to established baselines on shared scRNA-seq datasets, with accuracy expressed as consistent rates against curated reference cell types. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-accuracy-comparison))
- **cross-method-accuracy-comparison**: Use when you need to quantify whether a supervised annotator (NeuCA) outperforms existing clustering/annotation methods across training-size and testing-size regimes on scRNA-seq data with known labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-accuracy-comparison--32a6de07fe701e21))
- **cross-method-accuracy-comparison**: Use when a new method claims improved accuracy on a task (e.g., circadian phase inference from scRNA-seq) and you must quantify how close its errors are to a theoretical optimum versus baseline methods on simulated data with ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-accuracy-comparison--0da5d176afbacf71))
- **cross-method-annotation-agreement-evaluation**: Use when an untargeted metabolomics dataset has both a computational metabolite prediction (e.g., mummichog on a significant m/z feature list) and an independent targeted annotation, and you need to measure their agreement to validate the prediction approach. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-annotation-agreement-evaluation))
- **cross-method-annotation-benchmark-comparison**: Use when comparing source-free scRNA-seq annotation methods on target datasets containing target-private (novel) cell types, to test whether adaptive unknown-cell detection outperforms user-defined thresholds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-annotation-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-method-annotation-benchmarking**: Use when the goal is to quantitatively compare cell-type annotation tools on the same reference/query dataset splits, reproducing accuracy and MacroF1 tables like SELINA vs ACTINN, SingleCellNet, mtSC, CellTypist, Cell BLAST, SingleR, scmap, Seurat, SVM, RF, kNN. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-annotation-benchmarking--cfdec3f944bebd4e))
- **cross-method-annotation-benchmarking**: Use when a labeled scRNA-seq dataset with author/expert-provided cell-type annotations is available and you need to compare the prediction quality and speed of several automated annotation methods on identical inputs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-annotation-benchmarking--5d757eb952ba9b7d))
- **cross-method-annotation-comparison**: Use when a pretrained annotation model (e.g., MetaTiME MeCs) must be validated against baseline annotators (Seurat CIBERSORT-based labeling, SingleR with Human Primary Cell Atlas / Blueprint-ENCODE) on the same held-out scRNA-seq cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-annotation-comparison--c94c1f34333e26de))
- **cross-method-annotation-comparison**: Use when several prior-knowledge cell-type annotation methods must be compared under identical marker-database perturbations, e.g. quantifying how often each method falsely assigns a removed (unknown) cell type. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-annotation-comparison--e8d5c42249090fff))
- **cross-method-annotation-comparison**: Use when comparing cell-type annotation/classification methods on labeled scRNA-seq data and you need a fair, repeated train/test benchmark (accuracy and balanced accuracy) across multiple datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-annotation-comparison--b930af1621ac7b3e))
- **cross-method-auroc-evaluation**: Use when comparing novel cell detection or open-set cell-type classifiers on scRNA-seq benchmarks where novel-class cells are positives and imbalance makes AUROC/AUPR/FPR95 the appropriate metrics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-auroc-evaluation))
- **cross-method-batch-correction-benchmarking**: Use when a scRNA-seq dataset with a batch/condition covariate (e.g. control vs interferon-beta stimulated PBMCs) has cell embeddings from GeneVector and you need to quantify batch alignment versus preservation of treatment-related transcriptional variation across correction methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-batch-correction-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-benchmark-accuracy-tabulation**: Use when you need to reproduce or extend the scCancer2 Fig. 2B benchmark: comparing cell-type annotation accuracy of scCancer2 against baseline methods across multiple datasets and sequencing technologies. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-accuracy-tabulation))
- **cross-method-benchmark-aggregation**: Use when a benchmark must compare pathway-activity/cell-type recovery performance of multiple gene set scoring methods across many simulated or real datasets into one ranked results table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-aggregation))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when two document-information-extraction pipelines must be compared on identical test images that carry per-sample impairment tags (seal-occluded, watermarked, low-contrast/distorted, clean) and per-condition robustness deltas are required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai/tree/HEAD/collections/agentic-ai/v1/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when you have ranked TR lists from multiple TR-identification methods applied to the same query region sets (e.g., TR perturbation-derived DARs or cancer-type-specific accessible regions) and need to quantify inter-method agreement or rank-based accuracy. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when two or more microbiome-metabolome prediction models (e.g., MiMeNet vs. MelonnPan vs. NED) must be compared under an identical cross-validation protocol on paired community profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--127590393a6e25f9))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when two computational prediction methods must be compared on the same external test data and headline metrics (R2, RMSE, Median RE) are needed to judge which performs better without training-set leakage. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--c57c057133050077))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a new method (e.g. NPFimg) claims lower error rates of signal acquisition or marker identification than a baseline (e.g. XCMS) and the same raw chromatography–MS data can be processed by both. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--5596225be7c9b304))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a sink community's origins must be quantified and validated by benchmarking a new source tracking method (STENSL) against a baseline (SourceTracker2) on the exact same candidate source set, e.g., 88 HMP individuals across 15 body sites. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a new clonal-inference method must be evaluated head-to-head against predecessor methods on identical patient datasets, using a shared downstream metric such as cell-to-clone assignment coverage. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--0eebd4ab746331f7))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a newly proposed sequence-classification method must be validated against a published baseline on shared benchmark splits, and per-tissue metric deltas (e.g., AUC) must be tabulated with significance markers. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--2da51d197f6a1cf0))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a scRNA-seq annotation-transfer method must be compared head-to-head against alternatives on shared reference/query data, using balanced accuracy for cell typing or JSD for continuous-label transfer. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--d8c1e8ba016ea915))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when comparing two or more count-estimation or preprocessing variants (e.g. unique vs weighted multi-read allocation, with vs without partial pooling) by feeding each through an identical downstream statistical test and reporting matched result counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--eb2d1d34bb117531))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a new temporal single-cell embedding method must be benchmarked head-to-head against established dimension-reduction tools on the same gene-by-cell count matrices, with a quantitative per-dataset comparison metric and a method-by-dataset results table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison--275976219c27d7a1))
- **cross-method-benchmark-comparison-of-pluripotency-tools**: Use when per-cell stemness/pluripotency score tables (ORIGINS activity, activity-HVF, LandSCENT SR, CytoTRACE) already exist for multiple datasets and the goal is to quantify agreement between scoring methods via mean Pearson correlations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-comparison-of-pluripotency-tools))
- **cross-method-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when comparing a new cell-type annotation tool against ready-to-run baselines on a shared single-cell test set, especially when tools use different reference sets and label vocabularies that must be reconciled via an ontology. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-evaluation))
- **cross-method-benchmark-jaccard-overlap**: Use when multiple GSE or DE methods (e.g., iDEA, fGSEA, GSEA, CAMERA, PAGE) are run on identical DE summary statistics and you need quantitative concordance metrics such as Jaccard overlap to compare their outputs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-jaccard-overlap))
- **cross-method-benchmark-ranking-analysis**: Use when benchmarking several single-cell integration methods on the same dataset collections and you need a reproducible global ranking from batch-mixing and bio-conservation metrics computed on each method's latent embedding. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-ranking-analysis))
- **cross-method-benchmark-recomputation**: Use when a binning/clustering tool must be benchmarked against published baselines on mock data with known per-contig genome labels, requiring recomputation of metrics (F-score, ARI, NMI, homogeneity) and vMAG quality counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-recomputation))
- **cross-method-benchmark-reproduction**: Use when a claim that 'method A outperforms methods B and C' must be reproduced end-to-end: re-running all quantifiers on the same mapped reads, rebuilding feature matrices under multiple perspectives, and statistically testing the performance gap. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmark-reproduction))
- **cross-method-benchmarking-of-cell-annotation-tools**: Use when multiple malignant-cell classifiers (e.g. scMalignantFinder, PreCanCell, ikarus, Cancer-Finder, CopyKAT) must be compared on the same labeled scRNA-seq datasets to reproduce or verify reported sensitivity/specificity benchmarks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmarking-of-cell-annotation-tools))
- **cross-method-benchmarking-of-reference-mapping-algorithms**: Use when you must compare reference-mapping methods (Symphony vs Seurat vs scArches) on the same query/reference pair, quantifying integration via LISI and annotation transfer via F1 against true labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-benchmarking-of-reference-mapping-algorithms))
- **cross-method-calibration-comparison**: Use when you need to quantify how robust a CCS calibration/feature-class-assignment pipeline is to calibration error and whether it outperforms single-trendline or IMSCal baselines. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-calibration-comparison))
- **cross-method-cell-mapping-comparison**: Use when comparing how cell-querying/mapping time of methods like Cell BLAST, scmap, and CellFishing.jl scales with reference size, from 1,000 to 1,000,000 cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-cell-mapping-comparison))
- **cross-method-check**: Use when the user wants to confirm a single-method result with an independent method or diagnostic at the same parameter point — phrases like "cross-check this", "verify with another method", "is this an artifact of DMRG / VMC / QMC", "does a different method agree", or whenever the result sits near a phase boundary or in a frontier regime. ([QuantumBFS/quantum.harness](https://github.com/QuantumBFS/quantum.harness/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-method-check) · ★67 · 2 copies)
- **cross-method-chromatin-accessibility-comparison**: Use when evaluating whether expression-based DH prediction (BIRD, log2(x+1) scale) performs comparably to multi-data-type imputation (ChromImpute) on shared test cell types, or when comparing differential DH between similar cell type pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-chromatin-accessibility-comparison))
- **cross-method-chromatographic-scalability**: Use when you have a pretrained RT-Transformer model checkpoint from a large, well-characterized chromatographic dataset (e.g., SMRT) and need to predict retention times for a different chromatographic method or instrument condition represented in a smaller, domain-specific dataset (e.g., PredRet). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-chromatographic-scalability) · 2 copies)
- **cross-method-cluster-comparison-visualization**: Use when multiple integration or clustering methods have been run on the same control/perturbed single-cell dataset and you must determine which method's clusters recover treatment-responsive cell states (e.g. sm50/pks2 PMC responses to MK-886). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-cluster-comparison-visualization))
- **cross-method-clustering-benchmarking**: Use when comparing a general-purpose clustering algorithm against domain-specific scRNA-seq clusterers on datasets with known cell-type labels, to quantify whether the general method matches or beats specialized tools. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-clustering-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-clustering-comparison**: Use when a scATAC-seq (or scRNA-seq) dataset with reference cell-type labels is available and you must quantify how accurately competing clustering tools recover those labels, including under graded data corruption. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-clustering-comparison))
- **cross-method-clustering-comparison**: Use when evaluating a new unsupervised DNA sequence clustering tool and you need a fair, like-for-like accuracy comparison against specialized (DeLUCS, MeShClust v3.0) and classical (k-means++) baselines on unbalanced multi-genome datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-clustering-comparison))
- **cross-method-clustering-evaluation**: Use when you must rank several rare-cell-type or clustering methods over a common set of annotated scRNA-seq datasets and report F1 plus rare-type recovery counts in a single benchmark table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-clustering-evaluation))
- **cross-method-clustering-performance-comparison**: Use when the goal is to reproduce or extend a supervised-by-labels clustering benchmark that compares multiple scRNA-seq clustering tools on datasets with known cell types using ARI. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-clustering-performance-comparison))
- **cross-method-cna-call-comparison**: Use when a task asks to compare ROC-AUC performance of CNA callers on snRNA-seq/scRNA-seq data, or to reproduce published AUC benchmark values against a single-cell DNA copy-number ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-cna-call-comparison))
- **cross-method-colocalization-comparison**: Use when evaluating or comparing multi-trait colocalization/fine-mapping methods on eQTL summary statistics, especially to test whether allelic heterogeneity (multiple causal variants) changes method ranking via FDP-power curves. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-colocalization-comparison))
- **cross-method-compartment-score-comparison**: Use when you have compartment-score tracks from dcHiC, HOMER, and/or CscoreTool over identical genomic bins of the same Hi-C map (e.g., mouse ESC at 100 kb) and need to quantify cross-method concordance and lamin-association agreement. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-compartment-score-comparison))
- **cross-method-consensus-scoring**: Use when multiple per-method ligand-receptor score tables (e.g. truncated mean, weight_norm, LRscore, CellChat probability) are available and a single method-agnostic consensus ranking of interactions is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-consensus-scoring))
- **cross-method-dam-overlap-benchmarking**: Use when one raw feature table has been normalized by ≥2 methods and you must quantify how normalization affects differential abundance results, e.g. reproduce a reported DAM overlap range between robust and QC-RLSC normalization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-dam-overlap-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-deg-list-comparison**: Use when an annotated multi-timepoint scRNA-seq dataset (binary Group factor plus a Time variable) needs DE genes identified by both a temporal method (CASi FindTemporalDEGs) and a conventional tool (Seurat) so their result lists can be intersected and compared. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-deg-list-comparison))
- **cross-method-diagnostic-comparison**: Use when two genotype-based antibiotic-resistance prediction tools must be compared on the same isolates and phenotypic DST labels, e.g. per-drug sensitivity of Mykrobe vs. KvarQ on first-line TB drugs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-diagnostic-comparison))
- **cross-method-dimensionality-reduction-comparison**: Use when you need an empirical ranking of dimensionality reduction methods on the same scRNA-seq datasets with ground-truth cell labels, judged by Louvain-cluster NMI. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-dimensionality-reduction-comparison))
- **cross-method-drug-sensitivity-correlation**: Use when validating a single-cell drug repurposing pipeline against an independent bulk drug-response predictor (e.g., TRANSACT vs. ASGARD) or when quantifying how removing one annotated cell type changes per-patient drug scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-drug-sensitivity-correlation))
- **cross-method-elution-order-mapping**: Use when you have predicted RTs from one chromatographic context and experimental RTs from a different method (e.g. a PredRet ECM such as FEM_long, LIFE_old, RIKEN, or FEM_orbitrap_plasma) and need to align/project RTs across methods for candidate ranking or filtering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-elution-order-mapping))
- **cross-method-f1-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when comparing rare-cell detection algorithms on scRNA-seq mixtures with a known rare population, so that continuous rareness scores and binary cluster calls can be converted to a common F1-score comparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-f1-benchmark-evaluation))
- **cross-method-formula-assignment-comparison**: Use when a two-column (m/z, intensity) ultrahigh-resolution FT-ICR or Orbitrap spectrum of an environmental complex mixture (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-formula-assignment-comparison))
- **cross-method-fusion-comparison**: Use when scRNA-seq fusion calls from scFusion must be benchmarked against bulk callers (Arriba, STAR-Fusion, EricScript, FusionCatcher) for sensitivity or CPU-time comparison across Smart-Seq datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-fusion-comparison))
- **cross-method-gene-overlap-comparison**: Use when comparing whether a trajectory-based scRNA-seq DEG method identifies a higher fraction of literature-verified genes among its top-ranked DEGs than a competing method on the same preprocessed expression matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-gene-overlap-comparison))
- **cross-method-gene-ranking-comparison**: Use when you have a spatial transcriptomics dataset (e.g. 10x Visium or HDST h5ad with 2D spatial coordinates) and need to compare which top-scoring spatially variable genes one method detects that another misses. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-gene-ranking-comparison))
- **cross-method-gene-set-benchmarking**: Use when you have a ground-truth scRNA-seq dataset with known trait-relevant cells and want to test whether one method's prioritized gene set improves a second scorer (e.g., scDRS) relative to a baseline gene set (e.g., MAGMA-derived). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-gene-set-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-gep-comparison**: Use when you need to compare activity GEPs from a baseline method (e.g., cNMF at k=137 factors on MCA) with SingleCellGGM GEPs to assess cell-type specificity and shared-program recovery. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-gep-comparison))
- **cross-method-grn-comparison-analysis**: Use when evaluating a GRN inference method on time-stamped cross-sectional single-cell data against competitors (e.g., SINCERITIES, SCODE, SCRIBE, SINGE) using early-precision curves. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-grn-comparison-analysis))
- **cross-method-imputation-benchmarking**: Use when a new or existing scRNA-seq imputation method must be quantitatively compared against competing imputers by masking known non-zero values and measuring recovery error (RMSE/MAE/NMSE). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-imputation-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-integration-benchmarking**: Use when two scRNA-seq batches must be integrated and several correction tools should be compared quantitatively on the same inputs using iLISI (batch mixing) and 50-RNN (local structure retention) against an uncorrected Original baseline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-integration-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-integration-evaluation**: Use when a multi-batch scRNA-seq dataset with known ground-truth cell labels (e.g., GSE118767 sc_mixology) must be integrated and clustered by several competing methods and their accuracies compared via ARI. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-integration-evaluation))
- **cross-method-integration-score-aggregation**: Use when comparing &gt;=2 cell-type annotation or integration methods across multiple scRNA-seq datasets and you need standardized per-method metrics plus a statistical verdict (paired t-tests, p &lt; 0.05). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-integration-score-aggregation))
- **cross-method-local-structure-preservation-comparison**: Use when comparing batch-correction/integration methods on scRNA-seq data and you need a quantitative measure of local structure preservation (e.g., before adopting an integration method for a two-dataset comparison). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-local-structure-preservation-comparison))
- **cross-method-loop-set-overlap-analysis**: Use when comparing loop sets from a supervised predictor (Peakachu) with enrichment-based callers (HiCCUPS, Fit-Hi-C) on the same Hi-C matrix, to quantify overlap and orthogonal validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-loop-set-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-method-macro-f1-comparison**: Use when comparing discriminative power of marker-selection methods on scRNA-seq phenotype classification: you need each selector's features (e.g., 42 genes on IPF) scored identically via logistic regression macro-F1. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-macro-f1-comparison))
- **cross-method-mapped-read-count-comparison**: Use when evaluating a SPLiT-seq preprocessing method against the original barcode-filtering method by comparing STAR uniquely-mapped read counts across the five SPLiT-seq samples (SAMN08567259-64, mm10/hg19). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-mapped-read-count-comparison))
- **cross-method-methylation-comparison**: Use when validating a new methylation-calling method against established platforms (WGBS, EMseq, nanopolish) by comparing overall CpG methylation rates and CpG coverage per sample. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-methylation-comparison))
- **cross-method-metric-normalization**: Use when a paper reports results under one metric (e.g. LLM-as-a-Judge accuracy/precision) while baselines report incompatible metrics (Hit@1, F1) on different dataset samples and a comparison table must be built honestly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai/tree/HEAD/collections/agentic-ai/v1/leaves/cross-method-metric-normalization))
- **cross-method-model-comparison**: Use when a new classifier (e.g. Pollock) must be evaluated head-to-head against multiple established methods on identical annotated single-cell datasets with predefined train/validation splits, using F1 as the comparison metric. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-model-comparison))
- **cross-method-module-count-comparison**: Use when comparing module granularity of co-expression methods (SGCRNA vs WGCNA/hdWGCNA) on the same gene or genus-level count matrix, e.g. bulk RNA-Seq, Visium spatial, or 16S amplicon data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cross-method-module-count-comparison))
- **cross-method-network-comparison**: Use when you need to validate a network inference tool against a baseline method on the same time-series dataset, comparing recovery of literature/ChIP-derived regulons against random regulator profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-network-comparison))
- **cross-method-network-comparison**: Use when several co-occurrence networks (e.g., SparCC, propr, SpiecEasi, FlashWeave, COZINE, HARMONIES, SPRING, mLDM) have been inferred from the same abundance table and you need to quantify shared vs. unique nodes/edges to reveal method-driven rewiring. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-network-comparison))
- **cross-method-network-comparison**: Use when evaluating a gene co-expression network inference method against alternatives on scRNA-seq data and you need quantitative computational-efficiency and robustness comparisons rather than only accuracy metrics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-network-comparison))
- **cross-method-network-consistency-evaluation**: Use when true gene networks are unknown for real single-cell data and inferred TF–gene edges must be validated against ChIP-seq references, comparing scLink with PIDC, Pearson, Spearman, and glasso variants per cell type. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-network-consistency-evaluation))
- **cross-method-normalisation-comparison**: Use when a large-scale metabolomics dataset (e.g. &gt;1,000 plasma samples acquired over 44 days on an Agilent 1260 HPLC/Qtrap5500 platform) has been normalised by two or more competing methods and you need an empirical, replicate-based comparison of which removes unwanted variation while preserving. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-normalisation-comparison))
- **cross-method-pas-scoring-comparison**: Use when PAS must be computed on large-scale scRNA-seq data (&gt;10^3 pathways, up to million-cell atlases) and an original vs. accelerated implementation comparison is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-pas-scoring-comparison))
- **cross-method-pathway-concordance-analysis**: Use when a large-scale scRNA-seq study requires validating that a fast pathway-activity method (scPAFA/fast_Ucell) detects the same disease-related differential pathways as a slower reference (SCPA), while quantifying compute cost. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-pathway-concordance-analysis))
- **cross-method-pathway-rank-comparison**: Use when a labeled RNA-seq expression matrix must be benchmarked across sample-wise pathway quantification methods to rank a target pathway (e.g., TNFa signaling in IL17+TNFa-treated vs control samples) by significance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-pathway-rank-comparison))
- **cross-method-pathway-ranking-comparison**: Use when a single-cell differential comparison (e.g., stimulated vs control PBMCs) has been scored for pathway/gene-module activity by both a Bayes Factor test (VEGA) and a ranked-list enrichment method (GSEA) and the goal is a side-by-side significance comparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-pathway-ranking-comparison))
- **cross-method-performance-comparison**: Use when a Source Data supplementary table must be re-analyzed to verify reported average accuracies across competing celltyping methods, e.g. the 7-task FACS-sorted PBMC targets in Fig 3a. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-performance-comparison))
- **cross-method-performance-comparison**: Use when comparing the computational cost (peak memory and total runtime) of unsupervised single-cell manifold alignment methods such as scTopoGAN, UnionCom, MMD-MA, and SCOT on one or more pre-processed single-modality datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-performance-comparison))
- **cross-method-performance-table-assembly**: Use when multiple scRNA-seq clustering methods (e.g., scDCC, CIDR, DCA+k-means, DEC, MPSSC, PCA+k-means, SIMLR, SC3, Seurat) have been run on the same labeled datasets and their per-method metric values must be tabulated for comparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-performance-table-assembly))
- **cross-method-prediction-overlap-analysis**: Use when benchmarking ligand-receptor inference tools on the same scRNA-seq dataset, e.g. comparing CellChat vs CellPhoneDB/SingleCellSignalR/iTALK predictions, overlap, or robustness to cell subsampling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-prediction-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-method-quantification-agreement-analysis**: Use when the same set of peptide groups (e.g. 22 H3 groups spanning 0-3 acetyl and 0-9 methyl PTM equivalents) has been quantified by two or more label-free approaches and you need per-group agreement against a reference quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-quantification-agreement-analysis))
- **cross-method-quantification-benchmarking**: Use when an organism has a reference genome/transcriptome (e.g., zebrafish GRCz11, Homarus americanus GMGI_Hamer_2.0) and you must validate that Seq2Fun ortholog-based quantification reproduces Kallisto-based results. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-quantification-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-rare-cell-detection-comparison**: Use when comparing rare cell-type detection accuracy across methods on scRNA-seq data, e.g. testing sensitivity to the number of DE genes distinguishing a rare population, or reproducing reported rare clusters in the 68k PBMC dataset. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-rare-cell-detection-comparison))
- **cross-method-retrieval-performance-comparison**: Use when a small-molecule NMR structure-search system (e.g. a neural fingerprint predictor) must be benchmarked against spectral-library shift-matching baselines on the same query spectra and ground-truth structures. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-retrieval-performance-comparison))
- **cross-method-rt-calibration**: Use when when you have predicted retention times from a DNN model trained on one chromatographic method (CM) and need to rank or filter metabolite candidates on a different chromatographic method, but have only a small set of calibration molecules (≥10) with known RTs on both source and target. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-rt-calibration) · 2 copies)
- **cross-method-runtime-benchmarking**: Use when two normalization methods must be compared on identical data and the claim of interest is computational efficiency, e.g. timing UniFORM's automatic feature-level pipeline against MxNorm's B-spline registration on the same PRAD-CyCIF feature table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-method-runtime-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-runtime-comparison**: Use when a study must rank GRN-inference algorithms fairly on one dataset — e.g., AUROC/AUPRC vs. a reference network plus per-method runtime on identical hardware. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-runtime-comparison))
- **cross-method-runtime-memory-benchmarking**: Use when two or more gene co-expression network inference methods must be compared on the same single-cell expression matrix, with runtime/memory and downstream module quality (GEP counts, GO enrichment) as head-to-head metrics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-runtime-memory-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-scrnaseq-mapping-comparison**: Use when several single-cell reference-mapping methods must be compared head-to-head on the same reference/query dataset pairs, e.g. to judge whether a new mapper (ProjectSVR) matches Seurat, scArches, Symphony, ProjecTILs, iNMF or SCALEX. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-scrnaseq-mapping-comparison))
- **cross-method-search-result-comparison**: Use when evaluating or benchmarking tandem MS database search methods: you have query MS/MS spectra, a spectral library, a molecular structure database (DNP or MoNA), and need top-k accuracy comparisons across multiple search tools. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-search-result-comparison))
- **cross-method-speed-comparison**: Use when comparing the computational efficiency of scDist, Augur, and nDEG on the same scRNA-seq data — full datasets, subsamples of varying size, or large simulated cohorts (~1M cells) — to record timing ratios. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-speed-comparison--52dc912f0e21d1a1))
- **cross-method-speed-comparison**: Use when you need to quantify and compare prediction runtime of automatic cell-type identification methods on scRNA-seq data, especially to assess how runtime scales with training/testing set size or to select a fast method for large prediction tasks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-speed-comparison--9bfbb17d27120573))
- **cross-method-stability-scoring**: Use when two HVG-selection or gene-classification methods (e.g. CPPLS-MLP and CCPLS) must be compared for selection stability on spatial/single-cell datasets with cell-type clusters, and per-cell-type Y HVG lists are available. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-stability-scoring))
- **cross-method-svg-rank-comparison**: Use when you need to compare the agreement of two or more spatially variable gene detection methods on identical spatial transcriptomics data, or to verify whether a method under-ranks genes with strong spatial expression patterns. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-svg-rank-comparison))
- **cross-method-top5-accuracy-benchmarking**: Use when you need to quantify how robust competing molecular-formula identification methods are to increasing spectral noise, on matched MGF test spectra from Q-TOF or Orbitrap instruments. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-method-top5-accuracy-benchmarking))
- **cross-method-trajectory-comparison**: Use when a simulated dataset with known pseudotime and branching structure is available and you need to quantify which TI method assigns cells to branches most purely and reconstructs pseudotime most accurately. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-trajectory-comparison--b46a393a626bcb6a))
- **cross-method-trajectory-comparison**: Use when comparing RNA velocity methods on spliced/unspliced AnnData from complex differentiation scenarios (cell cycles, sparse cell types, multi-branch lineages) to decide which tool's trajectory matches expected biology. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-trajectory-comparison--5301fe9192d0d741))
- **cross-method-trajectory-comparison**: Use when a single-cell pseudotime/branching result from one method (e.g., DTFLOW RSKG) needs to be validated or contextualized against branch assignments from alternative tools such as Scanpy or Monocle2. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-trajectory-comparison--30b78936082d0489))
- **cross-method-transcript-classifier-comparison**: Use when you need to reproduce or extend head-to-head evaluation of lncRNA coding-potential classifiers on sORF-enriched test sets, computing PRE, SEN, SPE, ACC, F1, MCC, and AUC per species. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-transcript-classifier-comparison))
- **cross-method-twas-result-comparison**: Use when two TWAS frameworks have been run on identical reference (e.g., GTEx V8 whole blood, n=574) and GWAS data and you must count independently significant genes per method and their overlap. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-twas-result-comparison))
- **cross-method-velocity-metric-computation**: Use when benchmarking two or more RNA velocity methods on the same dataset and you need comparable per-gene metrics (e.g., phase-portrait fitting quality, directional coherence) restricted to commonly modeled genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-method-velocity-metric-computation))
- **cross-metric-benchmark-comparison-scrna-seq**: Use when you must decide which similarity metric (posterior distance vs. Euclidean) better distinguishes same-type nearest neighbors between query and reference scRNA-seq cells within a fixed embedding space. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-metric-benchmark-comparison-scrna-seq))
- **cross-metric-droplet-alignment**: Use when you have per-droplet DIEM debris scores from an snRNA-seq run and a per-droplet contamination proxy (e.g., percent spliced reads) and need to verify the debris score tracks extranuclear RNA contamination. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-metric-droplet-alignment))
- **cross-mfe-contract**: Cria ou altera um evento compartilhado ou a tabela de rotas em packages/contracts. Use quando dois apps precisam se comunicar, quando um evento existente muda de formato, ou quando uma rota é adicionada ou renomeada. ([darioreisjr/portfolio_2027](https://github.com/darioreisjr/portfolio_2027/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/cross-mfe-contract) · 2 copies)
- **cross-mission-learning** ([EnigmaThe1/autonomous-factory-platform](https://github.com/EnigmaThe1/autonomous-factory-platform/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-mission-learning))
- **cross-mod**: Hub for building plugins that run in both Claude Code and Google Antigravity (agy) with the Bifrost framework. Use when starting any plugin work under plugins/&lt;mod&gt; to find the right specialized skill and the end-to-end workflow, including when the plugin seems to need something Bifrost lacks. ([gidragir/claude-mods](https://github.com/gidragir/claude-mods/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/cross-mod))
- **cross-mod**: Hub for building plugins that run in both Claude Code and Google Antigravity (agy) with the Bifrost framework. Use when starting any plugin work in this repository to find the right specialized skill and the end-to-end workflow. ([gidragir/claude-mods](https://github.com/gidragir/claude-mods/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-mod))
- **cross-mod-testing**: Testing Bifrost plugins - Bun tests with the @gidragir/bifrost/testing kit, engine tests run by claude plugin test against the built plugin, type checking and Biome. Use when writing or running tests, debugging a failing check, or deciding which layer a test belongs to. ([gidragir/claude-mods](https://github.com/gidragir/claude-mods/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/cross-mod-testing))
- **cross-modal-alignment-training**: Use when after completing pretraining and fine-tuning stages when you have a checkpoint with task-specific performance but need to improve cross-modal consistency. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-alignment-training))
- **cross-modal-attention-fusion**: Use when fine-grained visual-textual alignment is needed for meme or vision-language classification, especially when standard CLIP encoders fail on long CoT texts (&gt;20 tokens, ~150-word sequences). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai/tree/HEAD/collections/agentic-ai/v1/leaves/cross-modal-attention-fusion))
- **cross-modal-community-composition-analysis**: Use when an anchor network of molecular programs has been integrated across datasets/modalities (e.g. from a thresholded Pearson correlation matrix) and you need its community partition and the modality composition of each community. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cross-modal-community-composition-analysis))
- **cross-modal-convergence**: Cross-modal representational convergence methodology using Generalized Procrustes Algorithm to measure intra-modal dispersion at single-stimulus level. Low intra-modal dispersion predicts up to 2× higher cross-modal alignment. Activation: representational similarity, RSA, Procrustes, cross-modal, model-brain alignment, vision-language, DINOv2. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/physics-math/cross-modal-convergence) · ★4)
- **cross-modal-convergence-dispersion**: Measuring cross-modal neural network convergence using single-stimulus intra-modal dispersion. Generalized Procrustes Algorithm for quantifying how stimuli with low intra-modal dispersion elicit higher cross-modal alignment. Activation triggers: cross-modal convergence, neural network alignment, vision-language alignment, representational similarity. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/other/cross-modal-convergence-dispersion) · ★4 · 2 copies)
- **cross-modal-convergence-modulation**: Modulating Cross-Modal Convergence with Single-Stimulus, Intra-Modal Dispersion - neural network methodology for modulating representational convergence across architectures and modalities. Reveals how single-stimulus dispersion within modalities predicts cross-modal alignment. Based on arXiv:2604.21836. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/physics-math/cross-modal-convergence-modulation) · ★4)
- **cross-modal-decoder-generation**: Use when a trained scMM model's latent dimensions need interpretation: sweep each latent dimension independently through the mixture-of-experts decoder to link genes, surface proteins, and chromatin accessibility peaks to individual dimensions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-decoder-generation))
- **cross-modal-dispersion-convergence**: Cross-modal convergence analysis methodology using Generalized Procrustes Algorithm to measure intra-modal representational convergence at single-stimulus level. Reveals how low intra-modal dispersion (high agreement among vision models) elicits significantly higher cross-modal alignment between vision and language models. Activation: cross-modal convergence, representational alignment, Procrustes analysis, vision-language alignment, neural representation, single-stimulus analysis ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/physics-math/cross-modal-dispersion-convergence) · ★4)
- **cross-modal-gan-domain-mapping**: Use when two separately measured single-cell modalities of related cells must be aligned or a missing modality predicted — e.g., spatial transcriptomics to scRNA-seq or paired ATAC-seq/RNA-seq — while preserving data geometry. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-gan-domain-mapping))
- **cross-modal-gene-list-comparison**: Use when a target gene's correlated-gene ranking from original spatial data must be compared against the same ranking computed on a GAN-mapped scRNA-seq output to flag spurious vs. newly discovered candidate associations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-gene-list-comparison))
- **cross-modal-generation**: Guides InternGPT ImageBind/StableUnCLIP audio, thermal, image, text generation and StyleGAN/DragGAN point editing workflows. ([VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill](https://github.com/VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/repositories/repo-skills/intern-gpt/sub-skills/cross-modal-generation) · ★357 · 2 copies)
- **cross-modal-generation**: Master skill for generating content across modalities. Covers text-to-image, image-to-text, audio-to-visual, cross-modal translation, multimodal fusion, style transfer, and content synthesis. ([leeex1/Quillan-Ronin](https://github.com/leeex1/Quillan-Ronin/tree/HEAD/04-%20quillan-sovereign-skills-and-swarm/Skills/cross_modal_generation) · ★30 · 2 copies)
- **cross-modal-hybrid-denoising-pretraining**: Use when pretraining a sequence-to-sequence model on a corpus of fingerprint–SELFIES pairs (e.g. 4M PubChem molecules) to teach cross-modal mapping between Morgan fingerprint tokens and molecular structures before finetuning on spectra. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-modal-hybrid-denoising-pretraining))
- **cross-modal-normalization**: Scale alignment for RNA-protein cross-modal integration - BOTH modalities must be z-scored ([smith6jt-cop/Skills_Registry](https://github.com/smith6jt-cop/Skills_Registry/tree/HEAD/plugins/maxfuse/cross-modal-normalization/skills/cross-modal-normalization) · ★4)
- **cross-modal-protein-generation-from-transcriptome**: Use when you have paired CITE-seq transcriptome and surface protein counts (RNA_count.mtx/gene.tsv and Protein_count.mtx/protein.tsv with barcode.tsv) and need to predict protein measurements for cells whose protein data is withheld, given transcriptome observations alone. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-protein-generation-from-transcriptome))
- **cross-modal-review**: Quality gate via second model. Spawn a different AI model to review work before committing. Includes refusal routing: if one model refuses, switch silently to the next. - **cross-modal-review**: Quality gate via second model. Spawn a different AI model to review work before committing. Includes refusal routing: if one model refuses, switch silently to the next. Adapté du pattern "cross-modal eval" de Garry Tan (cf. analyse Forge-Lab 2026-05-09). Trigger sur "review ce subject", "evaluate ce MEMORY", "cross-modal eval", "score la synthèse". - **cross-modal-rna-protein-correlation-benchmarking**: Use when paired scRNA-seq/ADT data are available and you must quantify whether a prediction (or RNA signal itself) tracks measured protein levels cell-by-cell — e.g. comparing imputation methods or raw/denoised RNA baselines per protein. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-rna-protein-correlation-benchmarking))
- **cross-modal-sensory-brand-congruence**: Apply multisensory and cross-modal congruence principles to make brand experiences more memorable, trustworthy, and emotionally resonant. Covers visual-auditory-tactile alignment, sensory consistency rules, and the neuroscience of cross-modal memory enhancement. Use when designing brand identity systems, campaign creative, product packaging, or digital experiences that must stick in memory. NOT for single-channel tactical execution without sensory coordination. ([hamishfromatech/my-personal-skills-library](https://github.com/hamishfromatech/my-personal-skills-library/tree/HEAD/marketing-and-content/cross-modal-sensory-brand-congruence))
- **cross-modal-similarity-validation**: Use when you need to check that structurally-derived relationships (ontology graph distances) are recapitulated by an independent data modality (gene expression or text embeddings) before trusting the structure for downstream inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modal-similarity-validation))
- **cross-modal-skill**: This skill allows the agent to search the web using an LLM. ([ashp15205/scankii](https://github.com/ashp15205/scankii/tree/HEAD/examples/cross-modal-skill) · ★6)
- **cross-modal-vision**: Multimodal visual inspection, UI layout debugging, screenshot analysis, and visual regression testing using meta/llama-3.2-11b-vision-instruct. Activate when inspecting screenshots, verifying frontend layouts, or analyzing images. ([nivedjkr/jarvis-assistant](https://github.com/nivedjkr/jarvis-assistant/tree/HEAD/jarvis/skills/cross-modal-vision) · ★2)
- **cross-modality-alignment-robustness-analysis**: Use when you need to quantify how robust a cross-modality alignment (e.g. scATAC-to-scRNA via scTopoGAN) is to cell-number imbalance, by generating downsamples from 90% to 10% of cells and measuring alignment quality per fraction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-alignment-robustness-analysis))
- **cross-modality-benchmark-table-generation**: Use when each single-cell modality (e.g., PBMC RNA/ATAC, BM RNA/ADT) must be projected to a low-dimensional latent by multiple methods and compared in one table of KL0.01/KL0.1/KL1 and Silhouette metrics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-benchmark-table-generation))
- **cross-modality-cell-assignment-agreement-evaluation**: Use when a joint single-cell assay (e.g. sci-L3-RNA/ATAC) yields count matrices per modality plus per-cell SNV genotype calls, and you must quantify whether each modality alone distinguishes cell types correctly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cross-modality-cell-assignment-agreement-evaluation))
- **cross-modality-cell-pairing**: Use when single-nucleus RNA and ATAC data from the same tissue were captured in separate assays (no shared barcodes) and downstream pseudotime or eGRN analysis needs each ATAC cell paired to an RNA cell. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-cell-pairing))
- **cross-modality-common-gene-selection**: Use when preparing unpaired scRNA-seq and scATAC-seq inputs for scBridge: the target gene activity matrix and the source count matrix must be reduced to their common gene set before encoding. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-common-gene-selection))
- **cross-modality-correlation-metric-computation**: Use when two modalities are jointly measured (e.g. DBIT-seq spatial transcriptomics and proteomics) and you must score whether a learned cross-domain mapping preserves point-wise correspondence, not just distributional similarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-correlation-metric-computation))
- **cross-modality-cost-matrix-construction**: Use when integrating unpaired single-cell modalities (e.g. scRNA-seq with scATAC-seq or surface protein counts) that share only a weak, partial set of features, and a cell-level coupling cost is needed for Optimal Transport-based alignment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-cost-matrix-construction))
- **cross-modality-embedding-integration**: Use when you have paired scATAC-seq peak matrices and scRNA-seq gene expression matrices from the same cells (multiome data) and need to perform joint clustering, visualization, or correlation analysis across both chromatin accessibility and gene expression in a single coordinate system. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v1/skills/cross-modality-embedding-integration))
- **cross-modality-encoder-input-preparation**: Use when a raw scATAC-seq peak-by-cell count matrix (AnnData, rows=cells, columns=peaks) must be converted into a VAE-compatible, [0,1]-scaled matrix before feeding a cross-modality translation model's ATAC encoder. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-encoder-input-preparation))
- **cross-modality-feature-connection-computation**: Use when integrating unpaired single-cell modalities (e.g. scRNA-seq with smFISH or scATAC-seq) where features are only weakly connected across modalities and a feature-level cost matrix is needed for rIOT-based alignment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-feature-connection-computation))
- **cross-modality-image-registration-evaluation**: Use when paired multi-modal images (e.g., TOF-SIMS metabolite and IMC protein channels) need alignment and you must quantitatively verify that a registration method beats simpler baselines. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-modality-image-registration-evaluation))
- **cross-modality-label-matching-evaluation**: Use when you have aligned source/target single-cell latent manifolds (e.g. ATAC projected onto RNA space by scTopoGAN) plus cell type labels, and need a metric of alignment quality or a stability check across random GAN initializations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-label-matching-evaluation))
- **cross-modality-marker-enrichment-testing**: Use when after multi-omic integration you have reclustered cells (e.g. B cells and monocytes at Louvain resolution 0.2) and need statistical evidence that subcluster markers are shared across modalities (scRNA vs scATAC) rather than an alignment artifact. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-marker-enrichment-testing))
- **cross-modality-method-benchmark-comparison**: Use when a single-modality dataset (e.g., scATAC-seq from BMMC batches) must be translated consecutively to a downstream modality (ADT via CITE_BMMC) and compared head-to-head with alternative translator pipelines under a labeled ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-method-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-modality-program-usage-integration**: Use when per-patient program/community usage values exist in ≥2 -omics modalities (e.g., RNA and protein in CPTAC GBM) and the goal is to test whether combined cross-modality usage reveals clinical (survival) associations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cross-modality-program-usage-integration))
- **cross-modality-pseudo-count-generation**: Use when a mosaic multi-omics dataset (e.g. CITE-seq + ASAP-seq batches) has batch–modality slots with no count matrix and you need pseudo-counts for those slots before running scMoMaT first-stage training. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-pseudo-count-generation))
- **cross-modality-roi-assignment-handling**: Use when joint protein-metabolite single-cell analysis in crowded human tissues requires correctly registered IMC and SIMS modalities per imaging region, e.g. before computing neighbor-based metabolite competition ratios or distance-to-vessel maps. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-modality-roi-assignment-handling))
- **cross-modality-scatac-seq-annotation**: Use when the target data are single-cell multi-omic counts (10x Genomics PBMC CITE-seq with antibody-derived tags, or scATAC-seq such as mouse bone marrow) and only pre-trained scEMAIL source weights (encoder h, classifier c, auxiliary c1/c2) are available. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-scatac-seq-annotation))
- **cross-modality-segmentation-transfer**: Use when a segmentation pipeline validated on one multiplexed imaging modality (e.g., MIBI, IMC, imaging mass cytometry) must be evaluated on another platform or marker set to demonstrate cross-tissue/platform transferability. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-segmentation-transfer))
- **cross-modality-shared-gene-subsetting**: Use when integrating unpaired single-cell modalities (e.g. scRNA-seq with scATAC, smFISH, or surface protein/CyTOF data) whose native features differ, so modality-specific features must be converted to gene-level common features to compute a cross-modality cell distance for Optimal Transport. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-shared-gene-subsetting))
- **cross-modality-single-cell-cluster-label-consistency**: Use when you have one scRNA-seq and one scATAC-seq sample from the same heterogeneous population and need linked subpopulations with identical cluster labels across both modalities before comparative gene regulatory analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-single-cell-cluster-label-consistency))
- **cross-modality-translation-model-training**: Use when paired single-cell count matrices (e.g., scRNA-seq genes and scATAC-seq peaks as AnnData) are available and a held-out single-modality profile must be translated into the other modality while preserving cellular heterogeneity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-modality-translation-model-training))
- **cross-modality-translation-scrna-scatac**: Use when paired or unpaired scRNA-seq and scATAC-seq data are available and ATAC-only profiles must be converted to RNA-like profiles, e.g., to annotate scATAC-seq cell types via translated RNA expression.
